=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N black balls and M white balls.
Each ball has a value. The value of the i-th black ball (1 \le i \le N) is B_i, and the value of the j-th white ball (1 \le j \le M) is W_j.
Choose zero or more balls so that the number of black balls chosen is at least the number of white balls chosen. Among all such choices, find the maximum possible sum of the values of the chosen balls.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
B_1 B_2 \ldots B_N
W_1 W_2 \ldots W_M

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N,M \leq 2\times 10^5
- -10^9 \leq B_i, W_j \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4 3
8 5 -1 3
3 -2 -4

Sample Output 1

19

If you choose the 1st, 2nd, and 4th black balls, and the 1st white ball, the sum of their values is 8+5+3+3=19, which is the maximum.

Sample Input 2

4 3
5 -10 -2 -5
8 1 4

Sample Output 2

15

If you choose the 1st and 3rd black balls, and the 1st and 3rd white balls, the sum of their values is 5+(-2)+8+4=15, which is the maximum.

Sample Input 3

3 5
-36 -33 -31
12 12 28 24 27

Sample Output 3

0

It is possible to choose no balls.

```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, final code can be:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    B = list(map(int, input().split()))
    W = list(map(int, input().split()))
    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)
    # prefix black
    prefB = [0]*(N+1)
    for i,b in enumerate(B, 1):
        prefB[i] = prefB[i-1] + b
    # prefix white positive
    prefW = [0]*(M+1)
    for i,w in enumerate(W, 1):
        prefW[i] = prefW[i-1] + (w if w > 0 else 0)
    ans = 0
    # k black chosen from 0..N
    for k in range(N+1):
        l = k if k < M else M
        total = prefB[k] + prefW[l]
        if total > ans:
            ans = total
    print(ans)

Code:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    B = data[2:2+N]
    W = data[2+N:2+N+M]
    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)
    prefB = [0]*(N+1)
    for i, x in enumerate(B, 1):
        prefB[i] = prefB[i-1] + x
    prefW = [0]*(M+1)
    for i, x in enumerate(W, 1):
        prefW[i] = prefW[i-1] + (x if x > 0 else 0)
    ans = 0
    # Since l = min(k, M), and prefW is nondecreasing.
    # We can iterate k.
    for k in range(N+1):
        l = k if k < M else M
        total = prefB[k] + prefW[l]
        if total > ans:
            ans = total
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

But before final, let's maybe think if there is a need to include `from sys import stdin` etc. Code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    B = data[2:2+N]
    W = data[2+N:2+N+M]

    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)

    prefB = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(N):
        prefB[i+1] = prefB[i] + B[i]

    prefW = [0] * (M + 1)
    for i in range(M):
        prefW[i+1] = prefW[i] + (W[i] if W[i] > 0 else 0)

    ans = 0
    for k in range(N + 1):
        l = k if k < M else M
        val = prefB[k] + prefW[l]
        if val > ans:
            ans = val

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Code:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    B = data[2:2+N]
    W = data[2+N:2+N+M]
    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)

    ans = 0
    sum_b = 0
    sum_w = 0
    for k in range(1, N + 1):
        sum_b += B[k - 1]
        if k <= M:
            if W[k - 1] > 0:
                sum_w += W[k - 1]
        total = sum_b + sum_w
        if total > ans:
            ans = total
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's decide final code with read all and cumulative. Let's write final code carefully:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    B = data[2:2 + N]
    W = data[2 + N:2 + N + M]

    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)

    ans = 0
    sum_b = 0
    sum_w = 0

    for k in range(1, N + 1):
        sum_b += B[k - 1]
        if k <= M and W[k - 1] > 0:
            sum_w += W[k - 1]

        total = sum_b + sum_w
        if total > ans:
            ans = total

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's double-check if `if k <= M and W[k - 1] > 0:` is correct. Suppose k <= M and W[k-1] is positive, add. Suppose k <= M and W[k-1] is zero/negative, no add. Since sorted descending, if W[k-1] is zero, later <=0, so no more positives. Good. If W[k-1] is negative, later <= negative, no more positives. Good. If W[k-1] is positive, add. Good.

But wait: What if W has positive values but there are zeros before positives? Sorted descending, zeros come after positives, so if W[k-1] is zero, all earlier are >=0, positives already added. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with `sum_w` not resetting for k > M? It remains sum of positive W among all M. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with `sum_b` adding B[k-1] for all k, including negative. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with `ans` initialized to 0, but if N=0? constraints N>=1, but if N=0, loop empty, ans=0. Good.

This frees the original list of references, but int objects remain because B/W reference them. Good. Let's include `del data`? It's okay. But if we use readline, no data. Let's choose readline for simplicity and memory. But readline assumes line structure. The problem statement gives line structure. I'd use readline to avoid data list and del. Code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    B = list(map(int, input().split()))
    W = list(map(int, input().split()))

    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)

    ans = 0
    sum_b = 0
    sum_w = 0

    for k in range(1, N + 1):
        sum_b += B[k - 1]
        if k <= M and W[k - 1] > 0:
            sum_w += W[k - 1]
        total = sum_b + sum_w
        if total > ans:
            ans = total

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This is clean. If input B line is very long (2e5 numbers), `input().split()` returns list of 2e5 bytes, map int, list ints. Fine. If there are trailing newlines, fine. If there are multiple spaces, fine. If the line is split due to line length? AtCoder doesn't wrap. But to be absolutely robust, use read all. I think either accepted. The problem statement says input is given in format, so readline okay. However, some judges may have line breaks arbitrary? Usually not; but using read all is safer. Let's use read all to avoid any line assumption. The code with data list is still simple. We can `del data` to reduce memory. Let's write:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    B = data[2:2 + N]
    W = data[2 + N:2 + N + M]
    del data

    B.sort(reverse=True)
    W.sort(reverse=True)

    ans = 0
    sum_b = 0
    sum_w = 0

    for k in range(1, N + 1):
        sum_b += B[k - 1]
        if k <= M and W[k - 1] > 0:
            sum_w += W[k - 1]
        total = sum_b + sum_w
        if total > ans:
            ans = total

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This is robust and memory okay. `del data` after slicing: B and W lists contain references to int objects; data list deletion doesn't delete int objects because B/W reference them. Good.
